[[general hustle and bustle]] Various discussions were held at the work level on the summer with the representatives of the telecoms industry, with the representatives of public and private broadcasting, the Federal Ministries involved, the existing authorities and the interest representatives. The result of these discussions is that a list of tasks has now been drawn up, which material-legislative competences are to be normized in this context. I believe that our government structure in general is doing a good job in this respect, but we must see that in the European Union the trend is quite clearly heading towards independent media authorities. It is therefore for us, in particular, in connection with these European objectives, on the basis of the list of tasks which has now been drawn up with all those involved, to come to a reasonable draft law based on broad consensus. I assume that, after these initial discussions and the preparation of the first proposals for a regulation, the discussion with Members here in Parliament is also being held, because of course, this is a highly sensitive matter. In the end, it is about protecting the diversity of opinion, which is one of the most sensitive goods in a democracy. Therefore, we have undertaken in the Federal Government to rely on a very, very broad consensus, because such an independent media authority ultimately lives on the general acceptance. I have the impression that the Federal Minister is on the way in this regard in her discussions. In the second part of Question Time, Mrs Minister, I am sure she will be at your disposal for further details, Mr Deputies.

ParlaMint-AT_2007-11-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00038_d7e3969	Please, Mr. Federal Chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-11-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00038_d7e3984	Ladies and gentlemen! The Austrian Federal Government has set itself the task of discussing the really broad package of proposals that the Convention has drawn up and trying to make proposals to Parliament on constitutional amendments. This summer, as the first part, we decided on the large package of electoral rights which the Austrian National Council has already decided. The second package was passed yesterday by the Council of Ministers, namely the package on constitutional adjustment, with about 1,000 constitutional provisions, including the rules concerning the new asylum court. This package will be available to the National Council for advice and decision-making this autumn. As a third package, we have envisaged the reform of the entire administrative jurisdiction for the beginning of next year, i.e. the introduction of the national administrative courts, the Federal Administrative Court and the like in order to achieve a more efficient organisation of administrative jurisdiction in Austria. In other words, we will try step by step to deal with this major conflict of the Constitutional Convention and to present it to Parliament in individual, objectively determined packages. As I said, the first two packages are completed. You can already look forward to the third package at the beginning of next year.

The Treaty of Lisbon, which we are discussing today, is a compromise, but it is the most important one that it represents an improvement in relation to the Treaty we have so far. The most important thing about the Treaty of Lisbon is that it exists at all, because it enables it to improve and make European policy possible. For seven years we had a standstill in the European Union with self-employment, discussion of the contractual foundations, a tough ringing all over Europe. These seven years are finally over, and the European Union can return to what is its task of making policy for the people of Europe. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Many European-skeptic citizens are disappointed – not because they reject Europe, Mr Strache, but because they want the European Union to work. [[Abg. Strache: Because they reject the contract and its consequences]] And the basis for it working, we are also laying with the Treaty of Lisbon. It is a question of people not rejecting the European Union – take note of this! – but instead want answers and solutions to the problems of time: What people want is a better Europe. The Lisbon Treaty provides an improved basis for this. [[Abg. Strache: People don't want any compulsion and no European...!]] Now it is about using the Treaty and seeing that we are promoting growth and employment policies in the European Union, moving forward with European infrastructure development, creating tools to combat global instability of the financial market and creating stability for our financial markets, meeting climate change, making globalisation positive, and reacting to international crises and threats together. Finally, the Treaty of Lisbon has also succeeded in anchoring the Austrian desire for a strengthening of the social dimension in the European Union. It is no longer true that only economic interest, the interest of the market, is given the decisive priority, but also that the social dimension and also the protection of public services has been enshrined in this contract. That was a big goal of Austria – the Federal Government, but also many countries and communities – and it is good that it has succeeded in anchoring it. The Charter of Fundamental Rights, on the other hand, not only gives us the opportunity to anchor individually indictable classical fundamental and human rights in the European Union, but also social fundamental rights are established in Europe. The most important thing, however, is, and this was also said to the ladies and gentlemen of BZÖ and FPÖ, that we recognise that the European Union is a political level, such as districts, cities, municipalities, countries and the federal government. It is a political level that makes policy, where there are good proposals and where there are bad proposals. [[Abg. We do not want a centralist state of European influence!]] It is about representing the interests of Austria at this level, and a good example of this is the solution that could be found due to the use of the Austrian Federal Government and the Federal Chancellor in terms of university access restrictions and access deadlines. One thing is also important to know: that without the EU, alone, all the problems facing the world today, we also had to deal with it, but we did not have an instrument to work on its solution at all. [[Abg. Strache: Switzerland is dealing with these problems perfectly!]] – Strengthening the international dimension is therefore an important point in the European Treaty. The High Representative, the EU Foreign Minister, is an important matter, but it is also important to say that the decision remains with the national state, because there is a principle of unanimity and therefore the Austrian foreign policy and neutrality policy are not even touched, just as the Sweden, the Irish, the Finnishs and the Malteses see. I would like to give an example because you mentioned the great Chancellor, foreign politician and internationalist Bruno Kreisky: Especially the foreign policy that Austria is now making – also in Chad on the basis of a UN mandate and on the basis of a decision by the EU foreign ministers – is an example of how to combine applied neutrality policy with solidarity and the possibility of helping people. Strache: The Kreisky would turn around in the grave!]] The Treaty of Lisbon will improve the European Union's ability to act, and this is important, because we do not want less Europe, but rather we want a better, a social Europe.

This means that the countries themselves can confess themselves to this by means of an instrument of direct democracy, namely the referendum in the country: I want to be 100 percent in the tightest circle of this module system to carry everything. Every other country, which is not economically as far as the population recognises and says that we do not want to go in all areas, is in a second area, an outer circle, inside and carries only certain modules. In the last area, an extreme circle, those members of this future European Union, designed in accordance with our concept, are located, practically only in a partnership for Europe, i.e. in particular, in specific areas, to conclude contracts with the European Union. ParlaMint-AT_2007-11-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00038_d7e14789	Madam President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Today, I can represent our Minister for the Interior Günther Platterhier, who is in Brussels with the Council of Ministers of the Interior and who has to deal with very important issues such as the upcoming Schengen enlargement, Blue Card, common European procedures in the field of deportations and other issues. I would also like to come here to answer the questions raised in the context of the urgent question. First of all, I should like to thank the honourable Member, Mr Westenthal, for his excellent report. I am not responsible for this portfolio, but what I know very well as a citizen and as a member of the Federal Government is: It is certainly the case that when there is unrest when there are potential dangers, it is not a matter of arousing emotions, but of maintaining peace, mindful and targeted action. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of SPÖ and Greens.]] That is an important point, I think, a fundamental consensus in politics. The essential thing is from our point of view: The right to freedom of assembly, which you have actually questioned in the heart, is an important fundamental democratic right, which is enshrined in the Constitution: Article 12 Basic Law, Article 11 of the European Convention on Human Rights. This is a cornerstone of our democratic order. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: But not for damage!]] It is not a question of whether individual demonstrations cause us great joy or not, but it is very likely that fundamental rights cannot be put into effect or out of date for the sake of lust and mood. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the SPÖ.]] This is an important point, my dear friend. Ladies and gentlemen, we should focus on this. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: There are prohibition rights!]] The right to freedom of assembly is, as has been said, a right set out in the Constitution. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, in this context we are dealing with clear analyses of the facts and facts. It is also because you have quoted this, always collected very precise information from the competent authorities and the police and assessed all the circumstances in the evaluation. An arbitrary prohibition, which is not actually justified, would be an unlawful interference in the constitutionally guaranteed fundamental right. Randomity does not produce security, and arbitrariness has certainly lost nothing in a rule of law. Therefore, it is clear: In order to prevent a demonstration, there must also be concrete circumstances which fear a threat to public security. Swammy and diffuse statements and arguments can at least be no reason. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: I will remind you on Monday, Minister!]] Now to the facts. – Determined by the Convention Act and the constitutional fundamental freedoms associated with it may be grounds for prohibition: First, if the congregation is to pursue or serve a criminal purpose. Examples: The meeting is to be attended exclusively or mainly by disguised or armed persons. Or it is an area demonstration that is organized for the purpose of disrupting another congregation. Or it is about spreading national socialist ideas. Secondly, if the Assembly threatens public security. Examples: If a meeting has already been notified and not been prohibited for the planned meeting place and time. The mere fear of an area demonstration is not enough. In such cases, the Authority shall, by appropriate means, protect the Assembly. Only if this is not possible and appears impossible despite appropriate effort will the planned meeting be prohibited to prevent a threat to public security. Furthermore, a ban will be considered if the organiser intends to provoke public rest disorder. If, on the basis of facts about the expected course of the Assembly, the Authority's forecast shows that the Assembly is at risk of public security, a ban will also take place. Therefore – and this is an important point –: It is observed by the competent authorities, essentially by the Federal Police Directorates, precisely, sensitive and detailed, and examined whether there are real reasons for a ban – before the demonstration and also during the demonstration. If there is concrete evidence, it will also be prohibited. It should also be said because such a loop was included in your explanations: There will certainly be no tolerance for those who actually commit acts of violence and interpret the right to demonstration for themselves to a right to violence and unrest. Here we will act consistently and also intervene. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] If there is a risk of public peace, order and security, then, of course, foreign police measures will also be taken. If one of these conditions is met, the corresponding measures on stay, i.e. the imposition of a ban on residence, shall be initiated. In the event that there is no willingness to leave voluntarily, then there is the possibility of compulsorily extradition, suspension of the drawer or relief, §§ 76 and 77 FPG. In the case of illegally detained aliens, the corresponding stay-ending measures shall also be initiated by the competent foreign police authorities and, if there is no voluntary return, by the corresponding foreign police force. The public construction sector alone has a volume of €30 billion, which is 11.7 percent of the total economy. The life expectancy of a building is relatively high at about 60 to 80 years, so investments in energy-efficient building design are worthwhile. Because the construction and operation of buildings consume a lot of energy. 75 percent of the space heat requirement in Austria is in residential buildings, 35 percent of the energy used is used for heating and hot water. Ladies and gentlemen, especially in the field of energy demand and energy efficiency of buildings, considerable success would be achieved, especially because the people are now very sensitive to it in the light of the climate change debate. Almost all households feel exorbitantly increased energy costs. With the prospect of low energy costs, therefore, many are quite willing to deal with resources more gentlely or to make energy-saving renovation investments. I believe we all agree that housing development in this area is one of the most important instruments when it comes to lastingly affecting the thermal-energy quality of buildings and their energy needs. In addition, however, we will also have to deal with structural measures to reduce energy demand in relation to spatial planning and land use. In this sense, I see the building culture report – like my previous speakers – as an important guide to policy, in order to examine and discuss its recommendations for their day-to-day implementation, because – the Secretary of State has mentioned this – the contents are of all our quality of life. - Thank you.

According to Vorarlberg Tourism office, about 20,000 nights are due to architectural travellers in the country. Since 1980, as part of European space policy and competitiveness, we have concluded a multilateral and intergovernmental agreement with all the ESA Member States at the time, which deals with relations between the participants in the Ariane development programme of ESA and the French private limited company. Arianespace's taking care of it. This company is responsible for the production, marketing and execution of the launch activities of the carrier rockets Ariane developed by ESA. An extension of the declaration on the production phase of the Ariane carriers by the end of 2008 by Austria is therefore necessary because it allows an interim solution allowing the participating States to negotiate a new long-term agreement on the deployment phase of the Arian, Vega and Soyuz carriers from the Guyana Space Centre. Austria does not thereby incur any additional costs, as the contributions to the Guyana space centre are linked to the ESA's budget. The Austrian interest in the extension by 2008 is to continue to participate in the worldwide marketing of the carrier rockets developed in Europe by the private law firm Arianespace, with these Austrian companies awarding contracts as subcontractors. There are very great successes and good experiences from the past in the research centre Seibersdorf. The development there also confirms the high orders, which are a lot more revenue than our share in the ESA's budget of about 1.3 percent, which Austria makes available. This project offers a substantial increase in revenue from orders. I would ask you to agree to this request.

Symposia, which has been dealt with in the course of survey events, is intended to expand the audit competence of the Court of Auditors, which works well. We are convinced that the Federal Court of Auditors could exercise a very preventive effect here, and by this prevention also the individual building agents, housing cooperatives, could keep an even more efficient and economic activity, because we also know that in the past there have been irregularities over and over again that there has always been the creation of reserves there or there, even where there was no need for housing creation. It is very useful if the Court of Auditors, with its competence – not only with its audit expertise, but also with its advisory expertise – is involved, offers assistance to these housing cooperatives, advises them, and thereby leads them to more efficiency in the interests of the owners of the housing. I am looking forward to the Committee's deliberations. Thank you.

Point First reading: Request by Dr. Reinhard Eugen Bösch, colleagues concerning a Federal Constitutional Act with which the Federal Constitutional Act, BGBl. No. 1/1930 is amended (394/A)
ParlaMint-AT_2007-11-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00038_d7e21968	We're now on the 9th. Agenda item. We are going into the debate. President. — The next item is the joint debate on the following motions for resolutions: 3 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-11-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00038_d7e21997	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! We call for a new Article 43a to be introduced into the Federal Constitution, which reads: “A referendum shall be subject to any consent from the Republic of Austria to conclude a contract of international law which will bring about an overall change in the Austrian Federal Constitution.” You may know that Article 43 B-VG provides for the holding of referendums on legislative decisions adopted by the National Council. This new Article 43a is intended to confirm that we believe – and that in fact all the representatives of the people should be of the opinion here – that international state agreements, which lead to a change of essential elements of the Austrian federal constitution, must also be subject to a referendum. We have already raised this issue in the debate on the new reform treaty. Some points have already been made by us, why we believe that this reform treaty must also be subject to a referendum, namely the simplified amendment procedure, the general authorisation to raise funds, the flexibility clause, the national competence of the Union, the priority of Union law and the loss of ever-increasing neutrality. If we take the priority of Union law, ladies and gentlemen, we must say: This reform Treaty points out that the priority of Union law will no longer be explicitly contained in the Constitutional Treaty, but that the fact that the principle of this priority will not be included in the future Treaty does not alter its existence and the existing case law of the Court of Justice. This one point should be recognised as an important argument on our part that we should be subject to a referendum in this reform treaty. The rule, rather Herbert Scheibner, that only a law that we decide by a sufficient majority can be subject to a referendum is a matter of course. This was the case this morning. This addition, which we wish to have with Article 43a, is intended to confirm this desire.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-11-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00038_d7e22035	The next item is Mr Wittmann's speech with 2 minutes' speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-11-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00038_d7e22056	Madam President. High house! I believe that this proposal is essentially empty, for the following reason: We have the possibility of a referendum on a law, as Mr Scheibner has already explained to you today, and this law is normally the empowerment law with which the Federal Government is authorized to sign something. Then you have the opportunity to between This empowerment law and the signature, that is, before the signature, a referendum is held in order to have a vote of the people that is to be taken into account in the signature for a yes or no. Following your request, this would be the following: The contract would be signed. This would have a valid international contract. And then there would be a referendum on whether this treaty is valid at all. That means that the Federal Government would commit itself – and after that one would have the referendum! I think the blamage would be disproportionately larger outside. The path we are now taking, namely empowerment law and, if necessary, a referendum between them, in order to make the signature according to the referendum, I think that it is much more reasonable and democratic.
